**Book 206:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 207:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas III* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Hobri, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-936-3. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 3 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table) plus one guide section per student chapter, with teaching guidance, misconception notes and answer keys; its Bab 1 answer-key tables (printed pp. 64–91) key every practice item, not only the chapter tests (printed pp. 1–230). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 208:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 209:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Bigger than and smaller than; biggest and smallest | Libya, Grade 1 pupil's book Pt 1, Unit 8 — Numbers to 20, pp.85–87 |
| Comparing numbers (to 100) | Libya, Grade 1 pupil's book Pt 2, Unit 16 — Numbers to 100, p.64 |
| Comparing two-digit numbers: compare tens first ('the stronger digit'), then ones; the sign's sharp end points to the smaller number | Armenia, Mathematics 2 Part I, p.8 |
| Series of two-digit numbers: the number line, unit steps, locating numbers on a line | Armenia, Mathematics 2 Part I, p.38 |
| Comparing (two-digit numbers) — L5 of the two-digit numbers lessons | Kazakhstan, Grade 2 (Ospanov set), L5 |
| Comparing by largest place value | Egypt, Primary 2 Term 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), Chapter 6, Lesson 6-9, pp.94–95 |
| Comparing numbers within 10 — "Let's Compare Numbers" | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 1-12, printed p. 29 |
| The number line | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 7-3, printed pp. 76–79 |
| Which has more? Part 1 — comparing quantities | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 2 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 11-1, printed pp. 10–12 |
| Which has more? Part 2 | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 2 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 11-2, printed pp. 13–14 |
| Which is larger? Part 1 — comparing numbers | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 2 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 11-3, printed pp. 15–16 |
| Which is larger? Part 2 | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 2 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 11-4, printed pp. 17–18 |
| Number line up to 100 — more/less on the line; circle the bigger number; missing numbers in sequences to 100 | Egypt, Primary 1 Term 2 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 14-5, printed pp. 34–35 |
| Comparing numbers from the largest place; > and < | Egypt, Primary 2 Term 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 6-9, printed pp. 94–95 |
| Comparing a number with an expression as an inequality | Egypt, Primary 2 Term 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), Lesson 6-10, printed pp. 96–97 |
| Comparing natural numbers | Armenia, Mathematics 3 Part 1, Chapter I, §6, p.24 |

| Comparing numbers up to 1000 — comparing and ordering numbers up to 1000: the school's grade 1 has 158 pupils and grade 2 has 152 pupils — which grade has more pupils? The method: a. look at the number of places — the number with more places is bigger; b. compare the hundreds place values — the number with the bigger hundreds value is bigger; c. if the hundreds values are equal compare the tens place values; d. if tens are also equal compare the ones place values; 1 = 1, 5 = 5, 8 > 2, so 158 > 152; on the number line from 100 to 180, 158 is placed to the right of 152: of two numbers on the number line, the one placed on the right is the bigger and the one on the left is the smaller — so 158 > 152; compare the given numbers using the number line above; compare (… 256 and 310; 419 and 297; 709 and 714; 520 and 532; 342 and 345); find the hundreds lying between the given numbers (168 and 561; 657 and 901; 230 and 735; 241 and 812) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 93 |
| Comparisons, weight balance, digit puzzles — compare the numbers (248 and 271; 459 and 419; 920 and 902; 712 and 812; 517 and 437; 154 and 171; 452 and 552; 703 and 730); who travelled the long road? (two jumping diagrams, jumps of 25 dm and of 20 dm); discuss whether the statements are true (10 cm is longer than 1 dm; 7 dm is shorter than 2 m; 10 cm is shorter than 42 mm); in the first box 8 bags of apples were packed at 3 kilograms each and in the second box 6 bags of oranges at 4 kilograms each — calculate which of the two boxes is heavier; put the digit 3 on the right side and on the left side of the two-digit number 84 and place them: compare the resulting three-digit numbers (843 and 384); if 1 apple balances 5 plums: 2 apples balance how many plums' weight? 2 apples and 1 plum balance how many plums' weight? 3 apples and 4 plums balance how many plums' weight?; three teddy bears stand in a row and their positions are swapped to make three-digit numbers (the bears wear 3, 8, 5): to make the biggest number which teddy's position must be swapped, to make the smallest number which teddy's, and how many three-digit numbers can be composed by changing their positions? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 94 |
| Comparing four-digit numbers — which of the provinces Dornod (1259) and Töv (1217) has more grade 3 pupils? The rule box for comparing numbers: a. look at how many places there are — the number with more places is bigger; b. if the numbers of places are equal, compare the values of the top places — the number whose top place value is bigger is bigger; c. if they are equal, compare the next places' values in order; 1 = 1, 2 = 2, 5 > 1, so 1259 > 1217 holds; shown also on a number line 1200-1300: 1259 lies to the right of 1217; of two numbers on a number line, the one on the right side is bigger and the one on the left side is smaller; mark the numbers on a number line at their places and compare them (647 and 641; 3206 and 3216; 7325 and 7125; 5105 and 5150); five abacuses are pictured: write how many thousands (мян), hundreds (з), tens (а) and ones (н) there are in each number; write the numbers in order from biggest to smallest; a game "Compare the numbers": lay out a set of place-value cards face down; each player takes 1 card for each place from the pile and builds a four-digit number; write the built numbers in order from smallest to biggest (the example: 4000, 7000, 102 are combined and written 4712) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 17 |
| Number lines, digit puzzles — the venue sold tickets numbered from 1 to 4000 for the circus performance; the last three digits of the ticket a spectator in the front seats took are 789 — what is the number of the ticket the spectator took (3789)?; find and mark the given numbers on the number lines (four lines with different scales: 0-100 by tens; 0-100 by twenties; 0-100 by tens offset; 0-100: the numbers 13, 17, 39, 52, 60, 65, 71, 78, 84, 96; 1, 6, 25, 34, 40, 48, 52, 73, 87, 99; 13, 21, 30, 42, 55, 58, 64, 76, 87, 99; 50, 29, 75, 40, 45, 60, 58, 80 — some to be placed approximately); mark the numbers 3450, 3520, 3730, 3850, 3160 approximately on the number line 3000-3900: write the skipped numbers in words; complete the sentences (3450 < 3520; 3450 is … than 3520; 3520 is … than 3450; □ < □; □ is … than □); worked example 5: play in pairs with the digits 3, 6, 5, 4, 7, 2: the given digits are 3, 6, 5, 4, 7, 2; the odd ones among the given digits are 3, 5, 7; compose a three-digit number greater than 500 whose digits are odd: the smallest such three-digit number composed from them is 537; using the cards 1, 9, 6, 5, 2, compose the following numbers without repeating digits: a. the smallest three-digit number; b. the biggest four-digit number; c. the smallest four-digit number bigger than 3000; d. the biggest three-digit number smaller than 600 whose digits are odd | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 22 |
| Comparing by number line and by places — the tree-planting centre planted 80547 trees in the first year and 80561 trees in the following year: in which year were more trees planted? Compare the numbers in 2 ways: method 1: position them on the number line (on the line from 80500 to 80600 the bigger number lies on the right side, so 80547 < 80561); method 2: compare by the digits' place values (80547 against 80561: 8 = 8, 0 = 0, 5 = 5, then 4 < 6, so 80547 < 80561); a girl's note: "When the numbers of places are equal, compare the numbers starting from the biggest place, matching the places against each other"; discuss how to compare (a boy's box: when the numbers of places are equal — 287435 … 287475 gives 287435 < 287475, comparing place by place; a girl's box: when the numbers of places are different, the number with more places is always bigger — 115307 … 95381); an activity: use number cards and the place-class table and play in groups of 2-3: a. the players draw a face-down number card one by one and place the card in their own place-class table; b. once a number card is placed, it may not be moved again; c. the players draw cards in turn and fill each place of their own place-class table; d. compare the numbers formed: the one who formed the bigger number wins; a self-check: a. write three numbers bigger than 750060 and smaller than 760800; b. using the digits 4, 6 and 0, race to write four-digit and six-digit numbers; c. write the number sixty thousand twenty-one in digits, swap with your partner and check; d. break the number above into a sum of place addends | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 9 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 64 countries (Armenia, Libya, Egypt, Kazakhstan, Greece, Jordan, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, South Africa, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Vietnam, El Salvador, Cyprus, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Fiji, Honduras, Iceland, Peru, Serbia, Suriname, Samoa, Turkmenistan)**.**. Libya carries comparing from the first count — bigger and smaller at 20, comparing at 100. Armenia carries the rule itself — compare the stronger digit first — from two-digit numbers in Grade 2 to multi-digit numbers in Grade 4. Egypt carries comparing from Primary 1 (Japanese-collaboration series) — comparing numbers within 10 and the number line in Term 1, comparing quantities and numbers and the number line to 100 in Term 2 — to comparing by the largest place value in Primary 2 (the Primary 1–2 rows were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Kazakhstan carries comparing as its fifth two-digit lesson in Grade 2. Greece carries comparison and ordering to 200,000 in Grade 4. Jordan carries comparing numbers within thousands in Grade 4. Other read countries teach comparing inside their own Grade 1–4 books and join by merge.** Laos carries comparing from the first count — one-to-one matching, then <, > and = in Grade 1 — through ordering whole numbers, decimals and fractions in Grade 5 to comparing integers on the number line in Grade 6. Yemen carries comparing and ordering from <, > and = within 10 in Grade 1 through 1000, 10,000 and 999,999 to hundreds of millions in Grade 5. Indonesia carries comparing and ordering from comparing groups and numbers to 20 in Grade 1, through three numbers and to 100 in Grade 2, to 1,000 in Grade 3, 10,000 in Grade 4 and 1,000,000 in Grade 5, then comparing integers and rationals in Grade 7 (Merdeka) and comparing integers and fractions in Grade 7 (K13 Semester 1). Nicaragua carries comparing and ordering from to 10 in Grade 1, through 3-digit numbers in Grade 2 and to 10,000 in Grade 3, beyond 10,000 and to decimals in Grade 4, to fractions in Grade 5, and to integers and rationals in Grade 7. Ethiopia carries comparing and ordering from numbers to 20 in Grade 1, through comparing to 1000 by Ethiopian city distances in Grade 2 and to 10,000 in Grade 3, to comparing 54,367 with 54,369 in Grade 4. South Sudan carries comparing and ordering from ascending and descending order of 1–10 in Grade 1, through ordering 3-digit numbers in Grade 2 and comparing and ordering to 4 digits in Grade 3, to 5 digits in Grade 4 (4263 > 4193), six-digit numbers in Grade 5, and ordering to a million in Grade 6 (Unity 645,465 vs Eastern Equatoria 962,716). Two things are not cited here: the Grade 1 printed answer rows omit 10 from both orders — include 10 — and the Grade 6 Exercise 2 area question mixes square metres with square miles (both should be square kilometres), so it is not used; the Grade 3 guide's ordering keys for Activities 15–16 contain errors, so only the student book is cited. Iraq carries comparing and ordering from to 9 and to 20 in Grade 1 — ordinal numbers taught before comparing to 20 — through comparing and ordering to 999 in Grade 2 and to 9999 in Grade 3, to comparing and ordering within millions in Grade 4. Bangladesh carries comparing from sight and touch before numbers in Class 1 — including the 0–20 number line used for k more / k less (Ch. 11, printed p. 81) — through comparison by blocks and by place value with ordering in Class 2 and < and > in Class 3, to comparison, ordering, largest/smallest from digit cards and successor/predecessor in Class 4, and ordering integers in Class 6. One English-only misprint is never taught as fact: the Class 2 comparison lesson says "3 blocks of tens more in 325" (correct: in 354, printed p. 16) — the Bangla original is correct; teach corrected. Nepal carries comparing and ordering from before/after/between and greater/less/equal to 10 in Grade 1, through 3-digit ordering and comparing in Grade 2 (both books) and just-after/just-before with <, = and > to 9,99,999 in Grade 3 — teach the English “just before” worked examples corrected, the Nepali book has them right — to ordering integers on the number line in Grade 6. Cambodia carries comparing and ordering from <, > and = to 10 in Grade 1, through large numbers in Grade 6, to integers and rationals in Grades 7–8, with real numbers and order on the number line again in Grade 10. Kuwait carries comparing and ordering at every stage — numbers 1–5 in Grade 1 Term 1, to 100 in Grades 1–2, to 1000 and 6 digits in Grades 2–3, to millions in Grade 4, whole numbers and decimals in Grades 5–6, fractions in Grades 5 and 7, integers in Grade 6, and rational numbers with absolute value in Grade 8. Band C adds comparison and order of real numbers, with intervals, in Grade 9 Term 1. North Macedonia carries comparing and ordering at Grade 1 (2022) — more/less on pictured sets, the signs >, <, = to 5, one more / one less, comparing to 20 and ordering to 30. Iran carries comparing from more-and-less and the signs <, >, = in Grade 1 to ordering rationals in Grade 8 and real numbers in Grade 9.

Vietnam carries comparing and ordering up the whole primary climb — more/less/equal and comparing numbers in Grade 1, the number line and comparing two- and three-digit numbers in Grade 2, comparing within 10,000 and 100,000 in Grade 3, multi-digit comparison in Grade 4, and order in the set of natural numbers in Grade 6. Stocked 2026-11-11 by the Vietnam re-verification sweep merge.

Mauritania compares and orders from Grade 1 — equal/not equal and ascending/descending order in term 2, the signs =, ≠, <, > in term 3 — through Grade 2 (printed pp. 80, 82), Grade 3 (ordering to 999), Grade 4 (comparing with > and <, and with = and ≠, printed pp. 42–45) and Grade 6 (comparing and ordering large naturals, printed p. 14) (established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, which found this lesson stocking no Mauritania row at all).

Ghana also contributes to this lesson as curriculum, teacher and student-workbook (task-sheet) evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook — and Ghana is not counted in the taught-in figure above. (Ghana rows stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.)

China carries comparing and ordering from two-digit numbers in Grade 1 to numbers to 10,000 in Grade 2, and extends it to all real numbers on the number line in Grade 7.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries comparing and ordering from the Primer's bigger-and-smaller pictures through Grade 1's tens-then-ones rule to rational numbers on the number line in Classes 7–8 and real numbers in Class 9.

Syria carries comparing from matching pictured groups in Grade 1 — bigger and smaller in words first, the < and > signs arriving in Grade 2 through the fish's mouth — through place-by-place comparison to 9,999 in Grade 3 and 99,999 in Grade 4, the digit-count rule in Grades 5–6, and number-line comparison of integers and rationals in Grade 7. Syria's Grade 3 book also compares and orders same-denominator fractions here, by their numerators.

Saudi Arabia compares by matching sets in Grade 1, then by places — tens first, then ones — growing the comparison to three-digit numbers in Grade 2, table-based comparison in Grade 3 and alignment from the largest place in Grade 4, with the older 1445 Term-3 volumes witnessing the Part Two chapters. Kuwait gains further practice evidence from the teacher-made Grade 3 booklet misfiled in Saudi Arabia's record: largest/smallest-from-digits and ordering exercises, with the booklet's two direction-label errors (PDF pp. 24, 40) taught corrected, cited as a Kuwaiti school booklet, not as a textbook.

Rwanda teaches comparing and ordering at every primary stage — with <, > and = and ascending and descending order to 999 in Year 2 and to 10,000 in Year 3, on to integers, fractions with the same denominator and decimals in P4, 6-digit numbers and decimals in P5, and numbers beyond 1,000,000 in P6.

Sudan compares from Grade 1 — greater than, less than and equality, with the symbols printed reversed in its right-to-left layout and filed corrected at citation check — through ordering to 1000 in Grade 2, millions in Grade 4 and 5, integers on the number line in Grade 6 and rationals in Grade 7.

India stocks comparing-and-ordering from Math-Magic Class 1–2, 4 and Mathematics Class 6: bigger and smaller; putting page numbers in order; number between two numbers; comparing numbers by digit count, then by leftmost differing digit.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9): comparing from the first 'which is more' between 1 and 2, by number line and by places, up to comparing signed numbers on thermometers.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6) and *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7). From its books: from Grade 1: more / fewer by matching — comparing two collections by one-to-one matching with arrows: more, fewer, as many as (flores vs granos, black vs red beans); from Grade 3: comparing 6-digit numbers — comparing numbers place by place; the signs > and < stated correctly; digit puzzles (smallest 6-digit number < 100.002; values of A in 312.0A5 > 312.065; largest/smallest 6-digit numbers with distinct digits); from Grade 7: net change — Signed net variation: second-semester minus first-semester consumption 77,66 g − 78,09 g = −0,43 g, the negative sign read as a decrease.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: comparing numbers 1-3 through objects: more and less; equal, less and more (Lesson 6); the signs <, > and = (Lesson 7); practice using the signs <, > and =; comparing picture counts and numbers with <, >, =; fill in <, > or = between numbers to 8; fill in <, > or = between numbers to 9, including 0; comparing two sums with <, > and =; comparing picture counts; number pairs with <, >, =; comparing two-digit numbers: more or less shown with <, >, =; counting and comparing numbers to 90 with bundles of ten (Lesson 36); from Grade 4: review: comparing and ordering numbers (Lesson 1, continued).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Read and write numbers to 20: read/write numerals 0–20, numbers greater/less than a given number (answers 8…; Exploring numbers: more/fewer, between, tens and ones, ordinal numbers; key language 5 is more than 2, 2 is…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: order numbers at least to 20; numbers around us, house numbers in order (pupil book…; More … fewer: use "more" or "fewer/less" to compare two numbers and give a number between them; answers: more…; Numbers in between: number between two numbers on 0–20 lines/tracks; answers: between 10 and 14 = 11, 12, 13;…; Ordinal numbers: order at least to 20 using ordinal numbers 1st–10th and beyond, incl. coins in a race/order…; Unë tani di: revision game (hare and tortoise) combining more/fewer, between, ordinals and tens-and-ones…; Provov veten: compare two numbers with more/fewer, give a number between them, split two-digit numbers into…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: find 1 or 10 more/less within 0–30, estimate quantities that can be checked by…; Revision facts (this closing sheet is headed "Unë tani di" in the teacher's book, but the contents and the…. from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Ordering and comparing large numbers; Comparing and ordering fractions (number line); Comparing and ordering decimal numbers. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): : comparing numbers, using <, > and = (plans 8–9).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Representación en la recta numérica; Orden de los números enteros; from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Los números enteros (Z).

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Comparing natural numbers by digit count then digit by digit; from Grade 7: Comparing values of numerical and variable expressions by evaluation; from Grade 8: Comparing numbers: common fractions, decimals, ordinary fraction vs decimal.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part One: compare two pictured sets by counting elements (farm picture, five differences); greater than: compare two counts, "greater than" in words then with the > sign; less than: compare two counts with the < sign; equals: two counts are equal, written with the = sign; use >, < or = between two numbers; choose/draw to make a comparison true; ascending order defined (smallest to largest); count/write 1–9 ascending; ascending runs and arranging given numbers ascending; descending order defined (largest to smallest); count/write 9–1 descending; descending runs and arranging given numbers descending; the next number (one more), frog-jump number path; next number: write/choose the number after a given number; the previous number (one smaller); review: race positions (winner = first, last, third), mixed ordering and comparison signs; missing numbers on the number line to 19; next/previous within the teens; from Grade 1 Part Two: one-to-one matching of two sets (a flower for every bee?) as comparison; the next number within 10–20 (card game, counting on); next number as age + 1 (birthday candles problem); the previous number within 10–20 (spinner game, counting down); compare two numbers to 20 with >, < or = from pictured counts; compare sums and differences with >, < or = (e.g. 5 + 1 against 9 − 3); ascending order to 20: recognise ascending groups, count ascending 7–16 and 10–19; descending order to 20: count descending 15–8 and 20–12, order group scores descending; hundred-chart work (Table of 99): from 70, the number 10 less and 10 more.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires comparing and ordering numbers up to 999 and using the signs <, =, >; Class 2 extends this to 9,999, Class 3 to 99,999 and Class 4 to 9,999,999. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: order small numbers ascending and descending, written into train wagons (recall of two-digit ordering); compare three game scores (37, 18, 42): most, fewest, and order largest to smallest; increased or decreased? compare a before/after pair of counts (20→30 sharpeners, 30→24 lemons, 60→107 almonds, 100→126 buttons); compare numbers on a 1–180 number chart: before/after, smaller/greater, and between (78, 105, 177); the symbols >, < and = named; compare on a number line in tens (220 vs 260, 260 vs 290) and chain three numbers with signs: 220 < 260 < 290 and 290 > 260 > 220, with how each is read aloud; ordering game: numbers written on cardboard cups are lined up largest-to-smallest or smallest-to-largest in groups and peer-checked; use <, >, = between pairs (200 vs 368, 406 vs 406, 724 vs 726 …); order 93, 193, 493, 793 with signs; compare own textbooks by page count: most pages, fewest pages, order the counts with symbols; from Grade 7: Integer comparison on the number line: a number is greater than every number to its left; Ordering positive rationals and their negatives made by putting a minus in front of each (card activity); Rationals grow left to right on a horizontal number line; ordering rationals by their positions; Comparing rationals by equalising numerators or denominators or by converting to decimals; Choosing a comparison strategy: sign, whole part, closeness to 0, 1/2 and 1, equal numerators/denominators, decimal form; Density: between any two rational numbers there is at least one more rational number, found by repeated halving/subdividing; Writing several rationals between two given rationals; integers that can stand in a bounded rational interval.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: forming the largest and smallest numbers from given digits; comparing and ordering fractions with a fraction table (wall).

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 1, Part 1 (student textbook-notebook, 2025); the Mathematics 1 methodological guide (teacher evidence); Mathematics 5 (student); Mathematics 6 (student): comparing objects: bigger, smaller; comparing quantities by making pairs (one-to-one matching); how many more, how many fewer; comparison (of quantities/numbers, review theme in digit context); comparison of numbers within 10 (plan extends comparison to within 20); comparing natural numbers; comparing natural numbers.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 20 teacher-evidence items from 8 of the guides (Book 1, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5, Book 7, Book 8, Book 25, Book 31; Grades 1, 2, 8, 9).

**Book 1:** *Le Monde des Maths 1, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1re année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 1,775,505 bytes, 201 PDF pages. The collection follows the revised curriculum/programmes of July 2020 (32 lessons in 6 units over 4 domains); items are the guide's 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 3:** *L'Explicite en Maths CP, Guide pédagogique* (CP = Grade 1) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,092,403 bytes, 116 PDF pages. The explicit-teaching method guide for the pioneer-schools programme; its Chapter 3 gives worked lesson sheets for the SAME 32-lesson Grade 1 programme as Book 1 — a shared programme, two different guides, cited separately, never merged. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 4:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 1, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,559,907 bytes; new edition September 2019, with ministry approval panel. Its contents table lists the same 32-lesson revised programme as Books 1 and 3, in Arabic; the PDF's legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so the contents were read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 5:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique* (CE1 = Grade 2) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,580,797 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 2 programme corresponding to Book 2. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 7:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2ème année du cycle secondaire collégial* (2AC = Grade 8, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 808,839 bytes, 93 PDF pages. Items are the distinct topics of the 2AC distribution, printed p. 82 in the shared programme chapter read in this guide. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 8:** *Switch On! 1re année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,979,650 bytes, 88 PDF pages. A 5-minute daily calculation-drill guide (Japanese method, dir. Prof. Akira Sato); items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire (tracks A and B, weeks 1–34). Its graphisme weeks 1–2 (pencil-stroke practice, no mathematics content) and its confirmation tests (assessment apparatus) are excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 25:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 3ème année du cycle secondaire collégial* (3AC = Grade 9, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 5,252,673 bytes, 215 PDF pages. Items are the distinct topics of the 3AC distribution (printed p. 92 in the shared programme chapter read in this guide). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 31:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذ(ة)* (teacher guide, Grade 2, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 55,959,139 bytes, 393 PDF pages. Items are the distinct rows of its yearly programme table (guide printed pp. 48–50, read from rendered pages), which states the Grade 2 content by domain with learning objectives — a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides, as the placements declare. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 8 items (8 from student books, 0 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S2, Book S3, Book S5, Book S6.

**Book S1:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 1.er grado, Tomo 1* (student textbook, Grade 1, Tomo 1, Unidades 1–5) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two part-files (LT1_01_01: 18,425,843 bytes, 103 PDF pp.; LT1_01_02: 4,635,219 bytes, 115 PDF pp.). Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book S2:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 1.er grado, Tomo 2* (student textbook, Grade 1, Tomo 2, Unidades 6–11) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two part-files (LT1_02_01: 11,881,880 bytes, 111 PDF pp.; LT1_02_02: 7,896,753 bytes, 110 PDF pp.). The tomo's printed pagination restarts; pages cited are its own printed folios.

**Book S3:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 2.° grado, Tomo 1* (student textbook, Grade 2, Tomo 1, Unidades 1–4) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); one file (LT2_01: 9,043,517 bytes, 164 PDF pp.). Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book S5:** *Libro de texto, 3.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 3, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 14,754,711 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book S6:** *Libro de texto, 4.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 4, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 21,073,518 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-evidence items from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

|---|---|
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (1r) comparing and ordering numbers up to three figures | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 24 |
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (2n) comparing and ordering numbers up to six figures | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 24 |

Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5 and Book 6.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Manual da 1.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 1) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Editora Moderna, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-267-0; 4,293,873 bytes, 81 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 2:** *Matemática — Manual da 2.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 2) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-297-8; 3,437,647 bytes, 56 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 23 calls the minuend “diminuendo ou aditivo” — “aditivo” is wrong for subtraction; the course teaches minuend. Editorial production notes are printed as lesson content on printed p. 50 — production matter, cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Moldova added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Moldova's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **12 books, all pupil textbooks: the Matematică / Mathematics student manual for each of Grades 1–12 (Republic of Moldova, in Romanian). There is no teacher guide and no syllabus in the Moldova Maths set in hand, so every Moldova row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Moldova counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 394 teaching items. The expansion map's “12 student + 0 teacher” is correct: textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files reconcile exactly at 12 distinct works — Grades 3–12 (10 books) recorded in books.json, Grades 1–2 as git-blob files recorded in textbooks.tsv. The old closed-52 inventory's “Moldova link-only, 0 in hand” is refuted: all 12 books sit secured in the tensae-code/liqlearns-textbooks GitHub repo and were read from there for this expansion (Grades 3, 4 and 6–12 via the mid-size-books-8 release; Grades 1, 2 and 5 via the Contents API on their textbooks.tsv git-blob paths), byte-verified — MD5s match books.json exactly for the ten Grades 3–12 books. The Grades 10–12 books are dual-track (profil real / profil umanistic) throughout; each section is stocked once, as one Moldova row, not once per track. Book errors: none found that changes a placement; the Grade 9 file's PDF p. 178 carries a stray page from another book (a biology text page) bound after the back cover in the publisher's print file — a file artifact, not part of the mathematics manual, cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the “Cine seamănă culege” review blocks, the Recapitulare / sistematizare / evaluare sections, the “Să recapitulăm” summaries, the recap exercise sets and “Test sumativ” pages closing chapters, and the “Răspunsuri (și indicații)” answer keys — are stocked nowhere, except where a review section is a topic's only statement, which is then marked as such. Printed-page bases: Book 1 — printed page = PDF page; Book 3 — printed page = PDF page; Book 4 — printed page = PDF page; Book 10 — printed pages as listed in the book's own Cuprins. Into this lesson the placements stock 7 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 3, Book 4 and Book 10.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

The Bahamas enters this lesson as curriculum evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every document in the Bahamas' in-hand set is a ministry requirement document (the 2010 primary guidelines, the 2003 BJC examination syllabus, the 2019 senior-high guidelines), not a pupil textbook or a teacher guide, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as teaching (the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent). The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-guide-evidence item from Book 2.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

| Using place value to compare numbers, with the < and > inequality symbols (M2.4) | Belize — *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed pp. 12, 19 |

Belize's class for Maths is PARTIAL (teacher-evidence-only): all 3 in-hand guides are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand, and the three *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* webs (Lower, Middle and Upper Division) sit at exact link only, unread.

## Bhutan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Bhutan's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **16 files: 13 student books and 3 teacher's guides of the Royal Government of Bhutan's *Understanding Mathematics* series (Department of School Education / Ministry of Education and Skills Development), English-medium throughout** — and placed 657 pupil teaching items and 30 teacher-evidence items. **Set correction, proved by the read: the expansion map's count of 10 student + 3 teacher is WRONG — the map counted only the textbooks.tsv snapshot (PP, I, II, IV–X) and missed the Class III, XI and XII student books recorded as secured and pushed in the staging books.json files and the push state; the true in-hand set is 13 student + 3 teacher = 16 files, every one grabbed and verified (byte size on every file; MD5 against books.json on the 11 files that carry one — all match; page counts match every record) and read in full.** The Class I staging-path collision is resolved: the main-branch grade-01 file is the student activity book (Book 3); the Class I teacher's guide (Book 4) is a separate, MD5-verified file that books.json records under the same staging path name. **Critical filing flag, carried exactly: both BHSEC filings claim "complete book, not a sample/preview" — FALSE. The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The PP activity book's Reproducible Pages (printed pp. 89–116, cut-out masters) and Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (pp. 117–132) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere; its assessment sheets renumber two chapters against the body (Data Management is Ch. 6 there, Ch. 9 in the body; Ordinal Numbers and Halves Ch. 11 there, Ch. 10 in the body) — body numbering is used throughout. The Class 1 activity book's Reproducible Pages (printed pp. 75–80) and Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (pp. 81–100) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The Class 2 activity book's Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (printed pp. 99–118) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The pupil Chapter 9 pages open at Lesson 3 (Interpreting Bar Graphs) — no pupil Lessons 1–2 for that chapter appear in the file; the guide's parallel survey and data-collection lessons stand as teacher evidence instead. Book errors in the Class 2 book are carried corrected: its imprint prints the copyright year as "20234", read as 2023 as on its guide, and its Chapter 6 Lesson 6 tells pupils to draw a cylinder and label its base and apex — a cylinder has no apex; the apex belongs to the cone in the same lesson. The Class 3 textbook's Chapter Overviews, Chapter Reviews and course-objectives/assessment front matter are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The Class IV textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class V textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 10 items (10 pupil) from Books 1, 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9.
